# Uncertain word meanings: AI triage and reviewer worksheet (20 November 2026)

**This is an AI triage, not a qualified review.** Six AI reviewers read each of the 1,358
uncertain meanings per language in its verses (`glosses.py context`) and judged whether the
Spanish and Portuguese render STEPBible's English gloss correctly. Every meaning is still marked
uncertain and AWAITING QUALIFIED REVIEW in the app; `reviewed.json` is untouched and only a
qualified reviewer signs items off there, by hand.

| | ok | fixed | for a scholar |
|---|---|---|---|
| es | 1277 | 52 | 29 |
| pt | 1291 | 36 | 31 |

- **ok**: the rendering follows the English gloss in its verse and reads correctly; the doubt left is the meaning of the Hebrew or Greek word itself.
- **fixed**: the rendering was wrong (meaning, agreement, tense, marks) and has been corrected in the batches (before → now below). The structural checks and the grammar audits (audits/run.sh) pass afterwards.
- **for a scholar**: STEPBible's English itself looks doubtful for the verse, or the AI could not judge.

How to use it: a reviewer ticks an item once satisfied and adds its id to `../reviewed.json` under the language. Full records (both languages, flag reasons, notes): `triage.jsonl`.

## For a scholar first

### Spanish

- [ ] `a6ecf50c01356f5b` H1464 גּוּד HVqi3ms/Sp3ms “[which] attacks us it” → **[que] nos ataca**. 
- [ ] `59f75fd1d93a1901` H1511 גִּזְרִי HC/Td/Ngmsa “and the Gizrite[s]” → **y el gerzita[s]**. 
- [ ] `19037e62ee73774d` H1656 גֹּ֫שֶׁם HVPp3fs/Sp3fs “rain its” → **su lluvia**. Ezek 22:24: the morph (HVPp3fs) is a Pual verb 'rained upon' (MT gushmah), but STEPBible's English reads a noun 'rain its'. The rendering follows the English faithfully; a reviewer should decide whether the English (noun vs verb) is right for this verse.
- [ ] `8e5c1b6479afb0d7` H1761 דַּחֲוָה AC/Ncfpa “and thrusting [women]” → **y [mujeres] que empujan**. Dan 6:18 Aramaic hapax dahavan; STEPBible's 'thrusting [women]' is an odd literal guess (alternatives: diversions, concubines, music, food). The rendering follows it, but the English itself is doubtful; a reviewer should decide.
- [ ] `c0098f4ec5e0a852` H2005 הֵן HTj/Sp1bs “if [was] I” → **si [estaba] yo**. Gen 41:17 hineni is 'behold, I [was]' (KJV 'behold, I stood'), but STEPBible's English gives 'if [was] I'. The rendering follows the English with a good [estaba]/[estava]; the reviewer should decide whether 'if' is an error for 'behold' (he aquí / eis).
- [ ] `637ea5143cec1d89` H2008 הֵ֫נָּה HD “<of> them” → **<de> ellos**. 1 Kgs 20:40 first hennah of 'here and there'; STEPBible's '<of> them' looks like a misreading of the adverb. The rendering follows the English; the reviewer should decide whether 'aquí' / 'aqui' is meant.
- [ ] `ee030021880bbc1c` H2008 הֵ֫נָּה HC/D “and them” → **y ellos**. 1 Kgs 20:40 hennah wehennah is the adverb 'here and there' (KJV), but STEPBible's English reads 'and them' (as the pronoun). The rendering follows the English (and uses masculine ellos/eles though hennah is feminine); a reviewer should decide whether to keep STEPBible's 'them' or render 'y allá' / 'e lá'.
- [ ] `828c89f0b7e113c9` H2186A זָנַח Hc/Vqw2ms “and it has excluded” → **y ha excluido**. Lam 3:17 wattiznach: morph says 2ms ('you have excluded'), but STEPBible's English gives 'it has excluded' (3fs, the soul). The rendering follows the English 3rd person; a reviewer should decide whether it should be 2ms (has excluido / excluíste).
- [ ] `8fe6f20c4660ae87` H2363A חוּשׁ HVqcc/Sp1bs “to agitate I” → **agitarme yo**. Job 20:2 chushi bi: infinitive with 1cs suffix, 'my agitation/haste within me'. STEPBible's 'to agitate I' is awkward and the rendering (agitarme yo / agitar-me eu) doubles the person; a reviewer should decide between a literal infinitive and 'mi agitación' / 'minha agitação'.
- [ ] `57d0db93b3dd40e2` H2398 חָטָא HC/Ncfsc “and you have sinned” → **y has pecado**. Exod 5:16: the morph is a noun (chatat 'sin/fault of your people', KJV) but STEPBible's English gives the verb 'you have sinned'. The rendering follows the English (2ms); a reviewer should decide whether to follow the noun reading.
- [ ] `5c3df042e42e8482` H2975G יְאֹר HRd/Npl “like the light” → **como la luz**. Amos 8:8: STEPBible's English 'like the light' reflects the consonantal text (kaʾor) where most read 'like the River (Nile)' (cf. the same verse's 'like [the] River of Egypt' and Amos 9:5). 'como la luz' follows the English faithfully; a qualified reviewer should decide whether STEPBible's gloss stands.
- [ ] `9a55883ce51d4b5d` H3319 שֵׁיצִי AC/Vep3ms “and it was brought forth” → **y fue llevada a término**. Ezra 6:15: Aramaic šêṣîʾ means 'was finished'; STEPBible's 'it was brought forth' is unusual. 'fue llevada a término' renders the sense 'completed' (agreeing with casa) rather than a literal 'fue sacada'. Reviewer: choose between following the literal English and the evident meaning.
- [ ] `4b873c1edda54e14` H3901 לָחֶם HNcmsa “bread” → **pan**. Judg 5:8: STEPBible glosses lāḥem as 'bread', but most take it as 'war/fighting (was at) the gates' (KJV 'then was war in the gates'). 'pan' follows the English; reviewer should decide whether STEPBible's choice is kept.
- [ ] `41487af9c49eee70` H4900 מָשַׁךְ HVqv2mp “they have drawn down” → **arrastrad**. Morph is a Qal imperative 2mp ('draw down!'), and the current 'arrastrad' renders the morph, while STEPBible's English says 'they have drawn down'. The English itself looks doubtful; a reviewer must choose between following the English (han arrastrado) and the form (arrastrad).
- [ ] `6aac95bf89fc494a` H5423 נָתַק HVpp2fs “I tore off” → **rompí**. Jer 2:20: morph is 2fs ('you tore off') but STEPBible's English is 1st person 'I tore off' (following the ancient versions/qere-like reading); 'rompí' follows the English. Reviewer should decide the person; 'arranqué' would also be nearer 'tore off'.
- [ ] `8235ba03b687753a` H5427 נֶ֫תֶר HRd/Ncmsa “with <the> soda” → **con <la> lejía**. Jer 2:22 'with the soda': 'lejía' is RV1909's traditional word, not the English 'soda' (natron); reviewer to decide 'sosa/natrón' vs keeping 'lejía'.
- [ ] `acf964136b1cba01` H5427 נֶ֫תֶר HNcmsa “soda” → **lejía**. Prov 25:20 neter 'soda' (natron); 'lejía' (lye) is the RV1909 tradition rather than the English 'soda'. A reviewer should decide between 'sosa/natrón' (closer to STEPBible) and keeping the traditional word.
- [ ] `71294663055a1244` H5462 סָגַר HC/Vqv2ms “and lance” → **y lanza**. Ps 35:3 segor: morph is a Qal imperative ('close [the way]') while STEPBible's English is 'lance' (a weapon noun). 'y lanza' reads as the noun (or the verb 'throw'!). The English looks doubtful against the morph; reviewer to choose.
- [ ] `7a701eab89a63273` H7280A רָגַע HVhc1cs “I will grant rest” → **daré reposo**. Prov 12:19 'ad-arg'iah is usually read "for a moment" (as long as I wink); STEPBible's "I will grant rest" (from the other ragha' root) looks doubtful here. The rendering follows the English; a reviewer should decide whether the English stands.
- [ ] `4107e62461198968` H7280B רָגַע HVhi1cs “I will make suddenly” → **haré de repente**. Isa 51:4 arg'ia: "I will make [it] rest/establish" vs "suddenly"; STEPBible's "I will make suddenly" splits the senses oddly. The rendering mirrors the English; a reviewer should check the English itself.
- [ ] `d8b86e691252ee03` H7462C רָעָה HC/Vqv2ms “and graze” → **y apacienta**. Ps 37:3 "graze (on) faithfulness": "apacentar/apascentar" means to tend/shepherd (the "cultivate faithfulness" reading), while "graze" is to feed (pacer / pastar). A reviewer should choose; "y pace"/"e pasta" would follow the English more closely.
- [ ] `591962371b072434` H7992 שְׁלִישִׁי HTd/Aompa “the thirty” → **los treinta**. 1 Kgs 6:8 has the ordinal adjective (Aompa) "the third [storey]"; STEPBible's "the thirty" looks like a slip in the English. The rendering follows the English; a reviewer should decide whether to keep it or use "los terceros/el tercero" / "os terceiros/o terceiro".
- [ ] `9054a3eedf9055b6` H8159 שָׁעָה HVhv2ms “smear” → **embadurna**. Ps 39:13 (not Isa 6:10 as the flag says): the hiphil imperative here is usually read 'look away from me / spare me'; 'smear' (shut the eyes) fits Isa 6:10 better. The rendering follows the English imperative correctly; a reviewer should decide whether STEPBible's 'smear' suits this verse.
- [ ] `cdd9ebeec7d42a79` G0498 ἀντιτάσσω V-PMI-3S “does he resist” → **resiste**. Jas 5:6: STEPBible's word order 'not does he resist you' suggests a question ('does he not resist you?'), but there is no '?'. The KJV reads it as a statement. A reviewer should decide whether to mark it as a question (¿resiste? / resiste?) or leave the neutral statement.
- [ ] `1c0a3568a0db1b93` G0568 ἀπέχω V-IAI-3S “was fully” → **distaba**. Matt 14:24 (variant ἀπεῖχεν 'was distant'): STEPBible's 'was fully' looks garbled. The rendering gives the sense 'was distant' instead of the English. A reviewer should confirm the English or accept distaba / distava.
- [ ] `c49f2f2276fc59e7` G1252 διακρίνω V-API-2P “were discriminated” → **hicisteis distinción**. Jas 2:4 διεκρίθητε: STEPBible's 'were discriminated' is ambiguous (you made distinctions / you were divided, wavered). The rendering picks the active 'made distinctions'. A reviewer should choose, since the English passive is unclear.
- [ ] `648a8b171670e828` G2148 Εὐροκλύδων N-NSM-T “[the] Northeaster” → **[el] Euroclidón**. Acts 27:14: the English translates the wind's name as 'Northeaster', but the rendering gives the Bible's own name (RV1909 Euroclidón / Almeida Euroaquilão). A reviewer should decide between that and a translation ([el] Nordeste / [o] Nordeste).
- [ ] `2d92d29e662ae61b` G3385 μήτι PRT-N “surely even” → **acaso**. εἰ μήτι = 'unless perhaps' (Luke 9:13, 1Cor 7:5, 2Cor 13:5). STEPBible's 'surely even' is unclear English; acaso / porventura give the Greek sense, not the English, and drop 'even'. A reviewer should decide (e.g. 'a menos que' / 'a não ser que').
- [ ] `d94e46180e68572b` G3989 πέλαγος N-ASN “then” → **entonces**. Acts 27:5: the word is πέλαγος 'sea', but STEPBible's English here is 'then', which looks like an alignment slip (likely δέ). The rendering follows the faulty English; a reviewer should probably give 'mar' / 'mar'.

## Fixed by the triage

### Spanish

- [ ] `2c7d43d8f2ece3c1` H0092 אֲגֻדָּה HR/Ncfsa “<into> a band” → **<en> una banda** (was “<en> un haz”). 2 Sam 2:25: the Benjaminites became one 'band' = troop of men; 'haz'/'feixe' (a bundle of stalks) takes the other sense of English 'band'. A group word follows the English and the verse.
- [ ] `1d5f2b94748e4cb6` H0150 אֲדַרְכֹּן HR/Ncmpa “of darics” → **de dáricos** (was “de dracmas”). Ezra 8:27: same as 1 Chr 29:7; the English says 'darics' and 'dracmas' renders a different coin. Keep the flag for the identification.
- [ ] `76dc9dea8d63080a` H0150 אֲדַרְכֹּן HC/Ncmpa “and darics” → **y dáricos** (was “y dracmas”). 1 Chr 29:7: STEPBible's English chose 'darics' (Persian gold coin); 'dracmas' renders the KJV/RV 'drams' instead. Render the English; the darics/drachmas question stays for review.
- [ ] `becacb2f3998d960` H0441A אַלּוּף HAampc/Sp1bp “cattle[s] our” → **nuestro ganado[s]**. Ps 144:14 allufim 'cattle/oxen' vs. 'chiefs'; the rendering follows the English, but with the bracketed plural only on the noun it reads 'nuestro ganados'; the possessive needs the plural too. Meaning stays for review.
- [ ] `e2e9e5c4e6d147f5` H0604 אֲנָקָה HC/Td/Ncfsa “and the gecko” → **y la salamanquesa** (was “y la lagartija”). Lev 11:30 anaqah, a small lizard; 'gecko' is salamanquesa (or geco) in Spanish, while 'lagartija' is also the rendering of the next word 'the wall lizard' in the same verse, so two different creatures read alike.
- [ ] `28b76f8b9c32fea9` H0691 אֶרְאֵל HNcmsc/Sp3mp “valiant one their” → **su valiente** (was “sus valientes”). Isa 33:7 er'elam: the morph is singular (Ncmsc) and the English 'valiant one their' singular (collective); the plural changes the number. The KJV's 'valiant ones' and the Ariel reading stay for review.
- [ ] `c6d8b50b1894ad99` H0890 בׇּאְשָׁה HNcfsa “foul weed[s]” → **mala hierba[s]**. Job 31:40 bo'shah, a stinking weed (KJV cockle). With the plural bracket on one word only, the plural reading gives a broken agreement (mala hierbas / erva daninhas); both words need it.
- [ ] `f1fc6fb5d9362e28` H0947 בּוּס HVtrfsa “kicking about” → **pataleando** (was “revolcándose”). Ezek 16:6,22 Hithpolel of bus 'kicking about/struggling' (trampled); revolcándose/revolvendo-se render 'wallowing', the KJV-like alternative, not STEPBible's English. The meaning dispute stays for review.
